From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.8, F33.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I came in because I’ve been really stuck on this feeling that I’m not actually cut out for what people seem to think I can do. Like at work, I keep thinking I’m not really senior material, and that somehow I’ve sort of fooled everyone into believing I can handle more responsibility than I actually can.\n\nIt’s not just a passing thought, either. I keep going over it in my head, beating myself up about it, like I’m waiting for someone to notice I’m not as capable as they think. And I know that probably sounds dramatic, because on paper I’m functioning, I’m showing up, I’m doing the work, but internally it feels like I’m constantly telling myself I don’t deserve to be where I am. That’s really why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge when thinking about being found out or not meeting expectations at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think “on edge” is probably fair, but it’s almost more like this constant self-accusing thing in my head. Like I’ll be at work, and even if nothing has actually gone wrong, I start thinking, “You’re not really senior material. You just somehow got people to believe you can handle more than you actually can.”\n\nAnd then I keep replaying it, like I’m building a case against myself. If someone gives me more responsibility, instead of feeling trusted, I’ll think, “They’re going to realize they made a mistake.” It makes me feel guilty, almost, like I’ve misrepresented myself somehow, even though I didn’t lie or anything. I just keep beating myself up over it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain tasks or responsibilities at work because of these feelings?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely avoiding work responsibilities, no. I’m still going in, still doing my job, still getting through the tasks. But I do notice I’ve become a lot more hesitant with things. Like, I’ll double-check myself more than I used to, sometimes even when I probably already know it’s right, because I don’t quite trust my own judgment lately.\n\nAnd it’s not just at work, honestly. I’ve kind of pulled back from friends and family too, not in some dramatic way where I disappear completely, but I’m less likely to answer messages or make plans. I think part of it is that I don’t really trust what mood I’m going to be in, or whether I’ll say something wrong, or make some bad call socially. So I’m functioning, but it feels like I’m doing it with a lot more second-guessing than before.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any changes in your mood, such as feeling sad or empty, over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I have. It’s been most days lately, I think. Not necessarily like I’m crying all the time or anything dramatic like that, but there’s this heavy, low feeling that kind of follows me around.\n\nIt’s like I’m still doing the routine — going to work, coming home, answering what I have to answer — but everything feels muted. Like the color’s turned down on the whole day, if that makes sense. I’ll be sitting at my desk or just at home afterward, and it’s not that something specific has happened right then, but I feel sort of weighed down and flat. It’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes and included symptoms like a racing heart or shortness of breath?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing, where it suddenly spikes and peaks within a few minutes. I do get anxious and tense, but it’s more like it builds and sits there for a while, kind of a background pressure rather than this abrupt wave of fear.\n\nI can’t really point to a time recently where I thought, “Something is happening to my body right now,” like a racing heart or not being able to breathe. If it has happened, it hasn’t stood out to me as a clear episode like that. It’s more ongoing worry and discomfort than a sudden attack, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of significant depression like this before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… looking back, I think I have. It’s not like one clean block where I can say, “It started on this exact day and ended here,” but these low periods have been coming and going for a while now.\n\nIf I really think about it, it’s probably been around eleven months, off and on. There have been stretches where I’ve felt more like myself, or at least more functional, but then the heaviness and the guilt kind of settle back in again. I didn’t really put it together at first because I kept telling myself it was just stress or a bad week, but it’s been long enough now that it feels like a pattern, not just a rough patch.\n\nDoctor: Has your low mood been making it hard for you to enjoy things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s actually been one of the more noticeable things, I think. I used to look forward to little stuff, especially on the weekends. Like, I’d usually be thinking by Thursday or Friday about grilling something, getting the charcoal going, making a whole afternoon of it. Or I’d go fishing if the weather was decent, even if I didn’t catch much, just to be out there.\n\nAnd music, too — I’ve got records I normally enjoy putting on after work or on a Saturday morning. That used to sort of reset me a little. But lately I’ll see the grill sitting there, or my fishing gear, or the records, and I just… don’t bother. It feels like too much effort, or maybe not even too much effort exactly, more like I don’t care enough to start. I keep telling myself, “Maybe tomorrow,” and then tomorrow comes and I still just leave everything sitting there.\n\nIt’s not that I suddenly hate those things. I know I used to enjoy them. I just can’t seem to feel much pull toward them right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where you feel somewhat better and your low mood lifts a bit, or has it been consistently low most days?\n\nPatient: It’s… mostly low most days, I’d say. I mean, there are moments where I’m distracted enough that I’m not actively thinking about it, like if I’m focused on some work task or just getting through dinner or whatever, but I wouldn’t say the mood really lifts in a meaningful way.\n\nLately it’s been this heavy, low feeling that kind of follows me around. Like I’m going through work and then coming home and doing the basic things I have to do, but everything feels sort of muted — like the color’s been turned down on the day. Some days are a little less bad than others, I guess, but it’s not like I wake up and feel clear or normal for the whole day. It’s more that the heaviness is there in the background most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year where you felt completely free of this low mood?\n\nPatient: Completely free of it… I don’t think so, no. Not in a way where I’d say, “Oh, I’m totally back to normal now.”\n\nLooking back over the last year, it’s more like these low, guilty stretches have been hanging around off and on for maybe eleven months. There have been times where it’s been lighter, or I could push it to the side a little better, and I might function more normally for a bit. But even then, it feels like it’s still sort of there in the background, waiting to settle back in.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is no — I can’t really point to a period where I felt completely clear of it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this current low mood that you're experiencing?\n\nPatient: This current low mood… I mean, if I’m counting the whole stretch, it’s been going on off and on for about eleven months now. Not every single day has been exactly the same, but looking back, those low, guilty periods have been hanging around for most of that time.\n\nIt’s hard to pin down an exact start date, because it didn’t feel like one morning I woke up and everything had changed. It was more like it crept in, and then at some point I realized, “Okay, this has been going on for a long time.” But yeah, roughly eleven months.\n\nDoctor: Are there activities that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things I notice the most, because it’s not like I had some huge exciting hobbies before, but I had things I looked forward to.\n\nLike on the weekends, I used to really enjoy grilling. I’d think about what I wanted to make, maybe get stuff ready earlier in the day, mess around with the charcoal or whatever, and it felt like a nice little ritual. Lately, I’ll have the grill sitting there and I just… don’t bother. It feels like too much effort, but also not even in a practical way — more like I just don’t care enough to start.\n\nSame with fishing. I used to like getting out, even if I didn’t catch anything. Just being there was the point. Now the gear just sits there, and I’ll think, “Maybe I should go,” but there’s no pull to actually do it.\n\nAnd music too. I used to put on records at home, especially in the evening, and kind of settle into that. Now I’ll see them sitting there and I just walk past them. It’s not that I hate those things now, it’s more like they don’t do anything for me the way they used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had an episode of depression like this before the one that started about eleven months ago?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anyone, I guess — times where I felt down or kind of stuck — but I don’t know that I’d say I had an episode like this before.\n\nThis one feels different mostly because of how long it’s dragged on and how hard it is to shake. Before, if I was feeling low, I think I could usually explain it by something going on, or it would pass after a while. I don’t have a clear memory of another stretch where I’d look back and think, “Yeah, that was the same thing.”\n\nSo… I guess the most honest answer is I don’t know. Nothing that stands out to me as clearly like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one episode of depression like this in your lifetime?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say that clearly, no. I mean, I’m hesitant to say “yes,” because nothing else really stands out in my memory as another full episode like this.\n\nI’ve had times in my life where I was down or discouraged, sure, but when you say “more than one episode like this,” I start trying to compare it to this past stretch, and I don’t know that I have another one that fits. This one has felt more persistent and noticeable to me. So I guess my answer is… I’m not sure, but I don’t think there’s been more than one that I’d recognize as being like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you had two or more episodes of depression in your lifetime?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can honestly say two or more, no. I know that probably sounds like I’m dodging the question, but I’m trying to be accurate.\n\nI’ve definitely had times in my life where I felt low or unhappy or kind of worn down, but when you say “episodes of depression,” I’m not sure where the line is supposed to be. Like, I don’t want to count every bad stretch or stressful period as something it wasn’t. This current one is the only one that really stands out to me as, okay, this has been a sustained thing and it’s affected how I’m functioning and how I feel day to day.\n\nBefore this, I can’t point to another time and say, “Yes, that was clearly another episode.” So my answer would be… I’m not sure, but I don’t think I’ve had two or more.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other periods of depression in your life before the one that started about eleven months ago?\n\nPatient: Before this one? I mean… not that I can clearly identify. I’ve had periods where I was unhappy or stressed or kind of in a rut, but I don’t know that I’d call them depression in the same way.\n\nI think part of what makes it hard is I don’t have a neat timeline in my head where I can point to a year or a few months and say, “That was definitely another depressive period.” There were times I probably withdrew a bit or felt discouraged, but they seemed tied to life stuff, or they passed, or I didn’t really think of them as anything specific at the time.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, not that I’m aware of — or at least nothing that stands out as a separate period of depression before this started.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling completely normal and without depressive symptoms for at least two months since this low mood started eleven months ago?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not for a full two months where I felt completely normal.\n\nIt’s more like… looking back, the low mood and the guilty feeling have been kind of hanging around off and on for most of these eleven months. There may have been better days, or even stretches where I was functioning more normally and not thinking about it every minute, but I wouldn’t say I had a solid two-month period where it was just gone and I felt back to myself. It’s been more uneven than that, but still sort of there in the background.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of significant depression before the one that started about eleven months ago that lasted for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not in the sense of a significant depression that clearly lasted two weeks or more.\n\nI mean, I’m trying to be careful because two weeks is not a very long time when you’re looking back over years, and I don’t want to accidentally erase something just because I didn’t label it that way at the time. But nothing really comes to mind where I can say, yes, that was a distinct two-week-or-longer period of being depressed before this current stretch.\n\nThere were probably rough patches, like I said, but they don’t stand out to me as the same kind of thing or as clearly lasting in that way. So my best answer is no, not that I can remember.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
